 for candidate in candidates:\n+        resolved = candidate.expanduser().resolve()\n+        if resolved in seen:\n+            continue\n+        seen.add(resolved)\n+        unique.append(resolved)\n+    return unique\n+\n+\n+def _env_first(keys: tuple[str, ...], env_file: Path | None) -> str:\n+    for key in keys:\n+        value = os.environ.get(key, \"\").strip()\n+ if explicit:\n+        return explicit.rstrip(\"/\")\n+\n+    direct = _env_first((\"REIGH_DATA_FETCH_URL\",), env_file)\n+    if direct:\n+        return direct.rstrip(\"/\")\n+\n+    base = API URL not found. Set REIGH_PAT or REIGH_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN.\")\n+\n+\n+def fetch_reigh_data(\n+    *,\n+    project_id: str,\n+    shot_id: str | None = None,\n+    task_id: str | None = None,\n+    timeline_id: str | None = None,\n+    api_url: str | None = None,\n+    pat: str | None = None,\n+    env_file: Path | None = None,\n+    timeout: float = 60.0,\n+) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+    endpoint = resolve_api_url(api_url, env_file)\n+    token = resolve_pat(pat, env_file)\n+    payload = {\n+        \"project_id\": project_id,\n+        **({\"shot_id\": shot_id} if shot_id else {}),\n+        **({\"task_id\": task_id} if task_id else {}),\n+        **({\"timeline_id\": timeline_id} if timeline_id else {}),\n+    }\n+    request = urllib.request.Request(\n+        endpoint,\n+        data=json.dumps(payload).encode(\"utf-8\"),\n+        headers={\n+            \"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {token}\",\n+            \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\",\n+            \"Accept\": \"application/json\",\n+        },\n+        method=\"POST\",\n+    )\n+    try:\n+        with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=timeout) as response:\n+            return json.loads(response.read().decode(\"utf-8\"))\n+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:\n+        detail = exc.read().decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n+        raise RuntimeError(f\"Reigh data fetch failed: HTTP {exc.code}: {detail}\") from exc\n+    except urllib.error.URLError as exc:\n+        raise RuntimeError(f\"Reigh data fetch failed: {exc.reason}\") from exc\n+\n+\n+def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:\n+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n+        description=\"Fetch canonical Reigh project data through the reigh-data-fetch Edge Function.\"\n+    )\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--project-id\", required=True, help=\"Reigh project UUID.\")\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--shot-id\", help=\"Optional shot UUID; {
```json
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/skills/reigh-data/SKILL.md\n+---\n+name: \"reigh-data\"\n+description: \"Fetch canonical Reigh project, shot, task, timeline, image, and video data through the reigh-app PAT-authenticated Edge Function.\"\n+---\n+\n+# Reigh Data\n+\n+Use this when an ArtAgents workflow needs live Reigh data. Do not query Supabase tables directly from ArtAgents: the canonical read path is the `reigh-data-fetch` Edge Function in `reigh-app`, which reuses the app's query shapes and ownership checks.\n+\n+## Environment\n+\n+Set a Personal Access Token and one URL:\n+\n+```bash\n+export REIGH_PAT=<personal-access-token>\n+export SUPABASE_URL=https://<project-ref>.supabase.co\n+```\n+\n+You can also set `REIGH_DATA_FETCH_URL` to the full function URL, or `REIGH_API_URL` to the Supabase base URL. `reigh_data.py` also checks nearby `this.env` and `.env` files, including the sibling `reigh-app` checkout.\n+\n+## Commands\n+\n+Fetch a whole project payload:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 pipeline.py reigh-data --project-id <PROJECT_UUID> --out runs/reigh/project.json\n+```\n+\n+Fetch one shot's app-shaped media and positions:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 pipeline.py reigh-data \\\n+  --project-id <PROJECT_UUID> \\\n+  --shot-id <SHOT_UUID> \\\n+  --out runs/reigh/shot.json\n+```\n+\n+Fetch task params/settings/output and timeline config/assets in the same payload:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 pipeline.py reigh-data \\\n+  --project-id <PROJECT_UUID> \\\n+  --task-id <TASK_UUID> \\\n+  --timeline-id <TIMELINE_UUID>\n+```\n+\n+The root launcher is equivalent:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 reigh_data.py --project-id <PROJECT_UUID> --shot-id <SHOT_UUID>\n+```\n+\n+## Payload Contract\n+\n+The response is intentionally a thin copy of the app's read surfaces:\n+\n+- `project` and `project_settings`\n+- `shots` plus `shot_settings`\n+- `shot_media.by_shot[shot_id].timeline_images`\n+- `shot_media.by_shot[shot_id].unpositioned_images`\n+- `shot_media.by_shot[shot_id].video_outputs`\n+- `project_media.items`, `project_media.images`, and `project_media.videos`\n+- `tasks` and `task_settings[task_id]`\n+- `timelines` with raw `config` and `asset_registry`\n+\n+Shot media preserves `timeline_frame` and derives `position` as `floor(timeline_frame / 50)`, matching the current app bucket semantics. Primary variant URLs are preferred over original generation URLs, matching gallery display behavior.\n+\n+## Guardrails\n+\n+- Always pass `project_id`. Optional `shot_id`, `task_id`, and `timeline_id` are filters inside that project, not standalone lookup keys.\n+- Treat `tasks[].params`, `task_settings`, timeline `config`, and `asset_registry` as Reigh-owned JSON. Do not rewrite their schema in ArtAgents.\n+- For new fetch needs, extend the Reigh edge function first, then update this client. Do not add direct table queries in ArtAgents.\n*** End Patch\n"
```
```json
}
```

What changed:
- Added PAT-authenticated Reigh Edge Function: [index.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/index.ts)
- Registered it in Supabase config and edge test config.
- Added tests: [index.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/index.test.ts)
- Added ArtAgents thin client/CLI:
  - [artagents/reigh_data.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/reigh_data.py)
  - [reigh_data.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/reigh_data.py)
  - `python3 pipeline.py reigh-data ...`
- Added ArtAgents skill subdoc: [SKILL.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/skills/reigh-data/SKILL.md)

Endpoint:
```bash
POST /functions/v1/reigh-data-fetch
Authorization: Bearer <PAT>
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "project_id": "...",
  "shot_id": "...",
  "task_id": "...",
  "timeline_id": "..."
}
```

It returns app-shaped data for `project`, `project_settings`, `shots`, `shot_settings`, `shot_media`, `project_media`, `tasks`, `task_settings`, and `timelines`. Optional IDs are scoped through `project_id`, so they cannot fetch cross-project rows.

Verification:
- `npx eslint supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch --ext ts` passed.
- New edge tests: `8 passed`.
- Nearby edge tests: `14 passed`.
- ArtAgents tests: `5 passed`.
- Deployed to Supabase project `wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`.
- Live PAT smoke passed against deployed function:
  - `has_project: true`
  - `has_task_settings: true`
  - `has_project_media_groups: true`

Note: both worktrees already had unrelated dirty changes. I left those alone.

- `reigh-app` included the whole `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator` stack, including sprint 2-8 commits plus the new Reigh data endpoint.
- `ArtAgents` included the cleanup branch contents plus the Reigh data tool and README/docs refresh.

Did they seem like they should be merged? From a git safety perspective, yes: clean fast-forward, no main commits lost. From a product/code-review perspective, I only deeply validated the Reigh data changes, not every older sprint/cleanup commit on those branches. - `ArtAgents`: `98bae15` -> `1208f38`

> DEVELOPER

can you see how i make a new effect on the front-end in reigh? Or a new animated sequence rather with remotion?

> AGENT

I’ll trace the Reigh video editor path for Remotion effects/sequences: where effects are registered, how clips reference them, and what files you’d touch to add a new animated sequence.

> AGENT

Yes. There are two different systems, and for a “new animated sequence” you probably want the second one.

**1. Clip effects**
These wrap an existing media/text clip or lower track content.

Files:
- [effects/index.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/effects/index.tsx)
- [effects/continuous.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/effects/continuous.tsx)
- [effects/entrances.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/effects/entrances.tsx)
- [EffectLayerSequence.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx)

Use this for things like `ken-burns`, `float`, `glitch`, entrances/exits. A clip references them like:

```ts
continuous: {
  type: 'glitch',
  intensity: 0.5,
  params: {}
}
```

For a built-in effect, add a component in `continuous.tsx` or `entrances.tsx`, then register it in `effects/index.tsx`.

There is also a frontend custom effect creator:
- [EffectCreatorPanel.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/EffectCreatorPanel.tsx)
- [useEffectResources.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useEffectResources.ts)
- [useEffectRegistry.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts)

That stores dynamic effect code as `resources` of type `effect`, then clips use `custom:{resourceId}`.

**2. Animated themed sequences**
This is likely what you mean. These are whole Remotion clip types like `section-hook`, `art-card`, `cta-card`, `resource-card`.

Reigh renders these through:
- [TimelineRenderer.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx)

It checks `clip.clipType`. If it is not a built-in type and exists in `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`, Reigh renders the registered Remotion component.

The registry is generated here:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-composition/typescript/src/registry.generated.ts`

Current source sequences live here:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-theme-2rp/src/effects/section-hook/component.tsx`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-theme-2rp/src/effects/art-card/component.tsx`

To add a new animated sequence:

1. Create a folder:

```txt
/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-theme-2rp/src/effects/my-sequence/
```

2. Add:
```txt
component.tsx
meta.json
schema.json
defaults.json
```

3. Component shape should match existing ones:

```tsx
import type {ReactElement} from 'react';
import {AbsoluteFill, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig} from 'remotion';
import {type EffectProps, composeAnimations, useTheme} from '@banodoco/timeline-composition/theme-api';

type MySequenceParams = {
  title?: string;
  subtitle?: string;
};

export default function MySequence({clip, params: rawParams, fps}: EffectProps<MySequenceParams>): ReactElement {
  const theme = useTheme();
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const {durationInFrames} = useVideoConfig();
  const params = rawParams ?? {};

  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{background: theme.color.bg, color: theme.color.fg}}>
      {/* Remotion animation here */}
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
}
```

4. Regenerate the registry:

```bash
cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-composition
npm run gen-registry
```

5. Then Reigh can render a timeline clip like:

```json
{
  "id": "clip-my-sequence",
  "clipType": "my-sequence",
  "track": "V1",
  "at": 0,
  "hold": 4,
  "params": {
    "title": "Hello",
    "subtitle": "Animated sequence"
  }
}
```

If the registry is not regenerated or the package is not installed/linked, Reigh shows the loud unknown-clip placeholder instead of silently rendering black.

So: for a reusable frontend Remotion “sequence/card/beat”, add it to `timeline-theme-2rp/src/effects/<clipType>/`, regenerate `timeline-composition` registry, then use that `clipType` in timeline JSON.

> DEVELOPER

Is there new UX to create an animated sequence on the front-end? Like a button next to the new text and new effect button that opens a window to AI generate a sequence based on images that i've selected optionally which it will use in the animation? Can you think through how this wouold work?

> AGENT

I’ll check the current editor controls around “new text” and “new effect” so the UX proposal lines up with what is actually present.

> AGENT

No, I don’t see a dedicated frontend UX for “AI generate an animated sequence” yet.

What exists now:
- Floating draggable **Text** button in [TimelineCanvas.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx:505).
- Floating draggable **Effect Layer** button beside it.
- `EffectCreatorPanel`, but that creates reusable clip effects that wrap content, not a full Remotion sequence/card.
- The renderer can already display generated sequence clip types through `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`, e.g. `section-hook`, `art-card`, `cta-card`.

So the rendering substrate exists. The missing piece is the authoring UX and generation path.

**How I’d Make It Work**
Add a third floating tool button next to Text and Effect Layer:

- icon: `Sparkles` or `Clapperboard`
- title: “Generate animated sequence”
- click opens a modal
- optional drag behavior could place the generated sequence at the drop position

Modal fields:
- Prompt: “Describe the animated beat”
- Duration
- Track / insertion time
- Sequence style/type:
  - Auto
  - Section hook
  - Art card
  - CTA card
  - Resource card
  - New custom sequence
- Selected references:
  - selected timeline clips
  - selected generation images/videos
  - optionally current shot images
- Toggle: “Use selected images in animation”
- Toggle: “Insert into timeline after generation”

Then generation flow:

1. Collect selected clips from timeline.
2. Resolve their asset URLs and generation IDs.
3. Call a new edge function, maybe `ai-generate-sequence`.
4. Edge function asks the model to produce one of two outputs:
   - simple mode: a timeline clip using an existing `clipType` with `params`
   - advanced mode: a new sequence component plus schema/defaults/meta
5. Frontend previews result in a Remotion player.
6. User clicks Insert.
7. Timeline gets a new clip:

```json
{
  "id": "clip-12",
  "clipType": "section-hook",
  "track": "V2",
  "at": 12,
  "hold": 4,
  "params": {
    "kicker": "2RP",
    "title": "New Renaissance",
    "subtitle": "Built from selected community images"
  }
}
```

For image-backed sequences, params should reference assets:

```json
{
  "clipType": "art-card",
  "params": {
    "title": "Featured Work",
    "caption": "Selected from the timeline",
    "imageAssets": ["asset-1", "asset-2"],
    "imageUrls": ["https://..."]
  }
}
```

**Important Design Call**
I would not generate arbitrary frontend code first. That is powerful but riskier and harder to validate.

Better path:
1. Start with AI generating `clipType + params` for existing themed sequence components.
2. Add new built-in sequence templates as code-reviewed components.
3. Only later allow “custom sequence code” generation, probably stored as a resource type similar to custom effects.

That limits deviation while still feeling like AI sequence creation.

**Files I’d Touch**
- Add button in [TimelineCanvas.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx:505)
- Add modal component, likely `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`
- Add timeline insertion helper similar to `handleAddTextAt`
- Add edge function `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence`
- Teach sequence components like `art-card` to accept image refs if needed:
  - `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-theme-2rp/src/effects/*/component.tsx`

The shortest useful MVP is: button opens modal, user prompt + selected clips go to edge function, edge function returns `{ clipType, params, duration }`, frontend previews it, then inserts it as a timeline clip.

> DEVELOPER

What should it have in common withn the existing effects thing? Can it use a theme? Do we have the idea of a theme? How should we include images/videos/assets - select them and they show as seleecte like they do in chat? Do they generate live and then preview + insert into the timeline? Can they copy and paste the same efect but with multiple images? How should they adjust the timing, etc.? Should we just edit with text via LLM for now or have some kind of UX? Maybe just edit the actual video for timing once it's on the timeline? Think it through, not MVP but super robust version that feels professional

> AGENT

Yes. The professional version should feel like the existing **Effect Creator**, but it should not be the same product. Existing effects are “wrappers around content.” Animated sequences are “timeline-native authored clips.”

**Core Model**
Use three related concepts:

`Effect`
Wraps an existing clip or lower layer. Examples: glitch, fade, ken burns.

`Sequence`
A self-contained Remotion clip with its own layout, timing, assets, and params. Examples: section hook, art card, carousel, product reveal, title beat, quote card.

`Theme`
A design system that sequences can use: colors, type, animation presets, layout rules, tone. Reigh already has the idea:
- `TimelineConfig.theme`
- `theme_overrides`
- `@banodoco/timeline-theme-2rp`
- `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`
- theme sequence clip types like `section-hook`, `art-card`, `cta-card`, `resource-card`

So yes, sequences should use themes. The generated sequence should either:
- use the active timeline theme, or
- let the user pick a theme if none is active, or
- inherit defaults from the project.

**What It Should Share With Existing Effects**
Shared:
- AI prompt + code/params generation flow.
- Remotion preview inside a modal.
- Parameter schema and controls.
- Save/update flow.
- Public/private resource model eventually.
- Error boundary / compile validation.
- “Insert into timeline” callback.
- Preview against selected media.

Different:
- Effects produce reusable wrappers: `children -> transformed children`.
- Sequences produce timeline clips: `{ clipType, params, assets, duration }`.
- Effects are category-based: entrance / exit / continuous.
- Sequences are format-based: title card / image story / carousel / quote / CTA / resource card / transition beat / custom.

**Sequence Creator UX**
Add a third button next to Text and Effect:

- Text
- Effect
- Sequence

The Sequence button opens a proper authoring modal, not just a chat box.

Modal layout:
- Left: prompt + selected assets + sequence type + theme.
- Center: Remotion preview.
- Right: editable structure/timing/params.

The selected asset area should behave like AgentChat attachments:
- thumbnail chips
- loading state
- image/video labels
- remove buttons
- order drag handles
- “Use selected timeline clips” button
- “Use selected shot images” button
- “Add from gallery” button
- “Clear” button

Asset order matters. For image carousels, resource cards, before/after reveals, and video-backed sequences, the order should directly drive timing.

**Generation Flow**
The robust version should generate in stages:

1. **Plan**
   The model returns a structured sequence plan:
   - sequence type
   - theme
   - duration
   - asset roles
   - beats
   - text fields
   - animation style
   - timing map

2. **Preview**
   Reigh renders that plan using existing trusted sequence components if possible.

3. **Refine**
   User can edit text, timing, asset order, style intensity, and layout directly.

4. **Insert**
   The plan becomes a timeline clip:
   ```json
   {
     "clipType": "image-carousel",
     "at": 12,
     "hold": 6,
     "params": {
       "title": "...",
       "beats": [...],
       "assets": [...]
     }
   }
   ```

5. **Timeline Editing**
   Once inserted, normal video editor controls own placement, trim, split, duplicate, track, and duration.

**Live Generation And Preview**
Yes: generate live into a preview draft, then insert.

Do not silently mutate the timeline on first generation. The professional feel is:
- Generate draft
- Preview it
- Adjust it
- Insert or replace selected sequence

After insertion, allow:
- “Regenerate this sequence”
- “Edit prompt”
- “Edit structure”
- “Replace assets”
- “Duplicate with new assets”
- “Detach to timeline clips” eventually

**Copy/Paste Same Effect With Multiple Images**
Yes, but call it a sequence preset/template, not an effect.

There should be:
- Duplicate sequence
- Replace assets
- Apply same sequence to selected assets
- Batch create sequences from selected images
- Save as reusable sequence preset

Example:
User selects 12 images and chooses “Apply Art Card sequence.”
Options:
- One sequence using all 12 images
- 12 individual sequences
- Groups of 3
- Match existing timing
- Fit to selected timeline range

This is where it becomes genuinely useful.

**Timing UX**
Use both simple and advanced timing.

Simple:
- Duration input
- Fit to selected range
- Beat speed: Calm / Normal / Fast
- Hold per asset
- Transition length
- Loop last frame

Advanced:
- Beat table:
  - asset
  - text
  - start
  - duration
  - animation
- Mini timeline inside modal
- Drag handles per beat
- “Distribute evenly”
- “Snap to music/beat” later
- “Respect current timeline selection duration”

But most timing adjustment should happen after insertion on the main timeline. The modal should tune internal beat timing; the timeline should tune clip placement and macro duration.

**LLM Text Editing vs UX**
Use both, with clear division.

LLM is best for:
- generating the initial sequence
- rewriting copy
- choosing a layout
- mapping assets to beats
- creating variations
- “make it punchier”
- “make this 2 seconds shorter”
- “use the third image as the hero”

UX is best for:
- duration
- selected assets
- asset order
- exact text fields
- theme
- insert/replace
- timing sliders
- preview scrub

A professional version should not be chat-only. Chat-only is too opaque for video timing. The right shape is a structured editor with an AI command bar.

**Recommended Product Shape**
Build `SequenceCreatorPanel` as a sibling to `EffectCreatorPanel`.

It should support:
- selected timeline clips as attachments
- theme selector
- sequence type selector
- prompt
- generated structured plan
- Remotion preview
- editable params
- beat/timing editor
- insert/replace/duplicate
- save preset

Data model should prefer structured plans over arbitrary generated code:

```ts
type SequenceDraft = {
  clipType: string;
  theme?: string;
  durationSeconds: number;
  params: Record<string, unknown>;
  assetRefs: Array<{
    assetKey?: string;
    generationId?: string;
    url: string;
    type: 'image' | 'video';
    role?: 'hero' | 'background' | 'texture' | 'reference';
  }>;
  beats?: Array<{
    start: number;
    duration: number;
    assetRef?: string;
    text?: Record<string, string>;
    animation?: string;
  }>;
};
```

Then later, if needed, add custom generated sequence code as a separate “Advanced custom sequence” path.

**Best Robust Direction**
Do not start by letting the LLM generate arbitrary Remotion code for every sequence. Start with a strong set of themed sequence components and let the LLM generate structured params/timing/assets for them.

That gives:
- predictable rendering
- professional UI controls
- theme consistency
- safer previews
- reusable presets
- much less broken generated code
- easier timeline editing

Then add custom code generation only for power users or internal tooling.

> DEVELOPER

Do we have a way to define/store theme for a shot alreaday? Do we need another interface for that? How would that work? I think having a style should probably be optional for generating an animagetion in this way though tbh - not always necessary

> AGENT

I’ll verify the current Reigh schema/hooks for project, shot, task, and timeline settings so this answer is grounded in what exists, not just the recent timeline theme fields.

> AGENT

Yes, we already have the storage primitives for shot/project “theme-ish” settings, but we do **not** currently have a first-class shot theme model.

**What Exists**
At the database level:
- `projects.settings` is `jsonb`
- `shots.settings` is `jsonb`
- `users.settings` is `jsonb`
- `useToolSettings` can read/write scoped settings across user/project/shot
- `update_tool_settings_atomic` explicitly supports `users`, `projects`, and `shots`

So we can store shot-specific animation/style settings today without a migration.

For video timelines specifically, there is already:
```ts
timeline.theme
timeline.theme_overrides
timeline.generation_defaults
```

That lives on the timeline config, not the shot. It is used by the Remotion themed timeline renderer.

For shot generation, the existing shot settings are mostly under:
```ts
shots.settings["travel-between-images"]
```

That stores video travel settings, LoRAs, motion settings, etc. It is not a clean “visual style/theme for this shot” object.

**Do We Need Another Interface?**
Yes, but probably not another table.

I would add a new scoped settings key, not overload `travel-between-images`.

Something like:

```ts
SETTINGS_IDS.SHOT_CREATIVE_STYLE = "shot-creative-style"
```

Stored at:
```json
shots.settings["shot-creative-style"]
projects.settings["shot-creative-style"]
```

Shape:

```ts
type CreativeStyleSettings = {
  themeId?: string | null;
  stylePrompt?: string | null;
  styleReferenceResourceIds?: string[];
  styleReferenceGenerationIds?: string[];
  colorMood?: string | null;
  typographyMood?: string | null;
  animationMood?: string | null;
  useForImageGeneration?: boolean;
  useForVideoGeneration?: boolean;
  useForSequences?: boolean;
};
```

The important part: this should be optional and advisory.

A sequence generator should work fine with:
- no theme
- no style prompt
- no reference images
- just selected assets and a user prompt

**How It Should Work**
For sequence generation, resolve style in this order:

1. Explicit choices in the Sequence Creator modal.
2. Selected timeline/theme if the timeline has one.
3. Shot creative style, if editing from a shot context.
4. Project creative style, if configured.
5. No style.

The UI should show that as a simple control:

`Style: None / Use timeline theme / Use shot style / Use project style / Custom`

Default should often be `None` or `Auto`, not forced theme. Your instinct is right: style should not be required for generating an animation.

**Theme vs Style**
I’d keep these conceptually separate:

`Theme`
A structured render system: colors, typography, sequence components, animation presets. Example: `2rp`.

`Style`
Creative guidance: “glossy editorial”, “grainy archive”, reference images, mood, art direction. It may influence prompts and parameter choices but does not need a registered theme package.

So for animated sequence generation:
- Theme controls available sequence templates and brand look.
- Style influences text, pacing, image treatment, motion, and choices.
- Neither is mandatory.

**Recommendation**
Do not store “theme for a shot” as a top-level `shots.theme` column yet.

Use scoped settings first:
```json
shots.settings["shot-creative-style"]
```

Then the Sequence Creator can read:
- current timeline theme
- shot creative style
- project creative style
- selected assets

And produce a sequence draft. If users start relying heavily on shot-level style, then we can promote it later into a more formal schema or UI surface.

> DEVELOPER

Is there any other functionality this overlaps with? Or data structure? Or something?

> AGENT

Yes. It overlaps with several existing concepts, so we should be careful not to create a parallel “style/theme/preset” universe.

**Overlaps**
`TimelineConfig.theme / theme_overrides`
Already controls themed Remotion timeline rendering. Sequence generation should read this when working inside the video editor.

`projects.settings` / `shots.settings`
Already the correct scoped storage place. We should use this rather than adding a new table for basic shot/project creative style.

`travel-between-images` settings
Shot-level settings already store generation mode, LoRAs, motion settings, prompts, structure videos, etc. The new sequence style should not get buried here unless it is specifically video-travel generation state.

`project-image-settings`
This already contains image-generation style reference machinery: reference images, reference modes, style strength, selected reference by shot. A new “creative style” model should reuse those references or point at the same resources, not duplicate uploaded images.

`resources`
Effects already use `resources` with `type: "effect"`. Style references and presets also use resources. For reusable sequence templates or saved style packs, `resources` is probably the right store.

`EffectCreatorPanel / effect resources`
There is overlap in AI generation, preview, parameter schema, save/update, public/private resources. But effects are wrappers; sequences are timeline clips. Shared infrastructure, separate UX.

`AgentChat selected attachments`
The attachment/chip model overlaps directly. Sequence Creator should reuse the same selected media attachment concepts so selected images/videos feel consistent.

`motion presets`
These are reusable motion configurations, mostly for video generation. Sequence timing/animation presets may overlap. We should either reuse the preset resource pattern or explicitly name the new thing differently, like `sequence-preset`.

`LoRA / style reference settings`
Creative style may include LoRAs or references. It should reference existing LoRA/resource IDs rather than copying values into yet another blob.

**Clean Mental Model**
I’d draw the boundary like this:

- `theme`: structured renderer package/design system.
- `style`: creative guidance and references.
- `preset`: reusable saved configuration.
- `effect`: transforms an existing clip/layer.
- `sequence`: creates a new timeline clip.
- `asset`: image/video material used by a sequence.
- `shot settings`: shot-scoped defaults and generation state.
- `timeline config`: actual renderable video state.

**Best Data Shape**
For one-off generated sequences, store everything on the timeline clip:

```json
{
  "clipType": "image-carousel",
  "params": {
    "title": "...",
    "assetRefs": [...],
    "beats": [...],
    "style": {...}
  }
}
```

For reusable styles/templates, store them in `resources`:

```json
{
  "type": "sequence-preset",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "Fast image carousel",
    "clipType": "image-carousel",
    "paramsDefaults": {},
    "schema": []
  }
}
```

For shot/project default style, store scoped settings:

```json
shots.settings["creative-style"]
projects.settings["creative-style"]
```

But do not make sequence generation depend on that.

**Main Risk**
The risk is creating four slightly different ways to say “style”:
- image-generation style refs
- shot travel settings
- timeline theme
- sequence creator style

The robust design should make Sequence Creator consume existing theme/style/ref data, not fork it.

> DEVELOPER

What actually makes sense for the video editor context though?

> AGENT

For the **video editor context**, the cleanest answer is: do **not** make shot theme the primary thing.

The video editor’s source of truth is the **timeline**, not the shot. A sequence is a timeline clip, so its style, timing, assets, and params should primarily live on the timeline clip or timeline config.

**What Makes Sense**
Use this hierarchy:

1. **Timeline theme**
   Stored on `TimelineConfig.theme` / `theme_overrides`.

   This is the right place for video-wide design language:
   - fonts
   - colors
   - brand package
   - sequence component registry
   - animation defaults
   - render style

   If the video has a theme, generated sequences should default to it.

2. **Sequence clip params**
   Stored directly on the inserted timeline clip.

   This is the right place for the actual generated sequence:
   - selected image/video asset refs
   - title/copy
   - per-beat timing
   - animation choices
   - layout variant
   - local style overrides

   Example:
   ```json
   {
     "clipType": "image-carousel",
     "track": "V2",
     "at": 14,
     "hold": 6,
     "params": {
       "title": "Featured Works",
       "assetRefs": ["asset-1", "asset-2"],
       "beats": [
         { "assetRef": "asset-1", "start": 0, "duration": 2 },
         { "assetRef": "asset-2", "start": 2, "duration": 2 }
       ],
       "styleOverride": {
         "mood": "editorial",
         "accentColor": "#ffcc66"
       }
     }
   }
   ```

3. **Project/shot style only as optional input**
   This can inform generation, but should not govern the video editor.

   Use it like:
   - “Use current shot style”
   - “Use project style”
   - “Ignore style”

   It should be a prompt/default source, not the saved truth for the generated sequence.

**Why**
The same shot images can appear in multiple videos with different styles. If style lives on the shot, changing the shot could unexpectedly change or confuse unrelated timelines.

In the editor, users expect:
- this timeline has a theme
- this clip has editable params
- this sequence can be duplicated/replaced/regenerated
- this timeline render is reproducible later

That points to timeline-level and clip-level storage.

**Recommended UX**
In Sequence Creator:

- `Theme`: Auto / Timeline theme / None / Custom
- `Style source`: None / Project style / Shot style / Selected asset style / Custom prompt
- `Assets`: selected timeline clips shown as attachment chips
- `Generate`
- `Preview`
- `Insert`

After insertion:
- macro timing is edited on the main timeline
- internal beat timing is edited in the clip inspector
- style changes become clip params or timeline theme changes

**Do Not**
I would not add a required “shot theme” interface for this.

I would not store sequence-specific style under `shots.settings["travel-between-images"]`.

I would not make generated sequences depend on mutable shot style after insertion.

**Best Product Rule**
When generating: shot/project style can be borrowed.

After inserting: the sequence owns its own params, and the timeline owns the theme.

> DEVELOPER

can you go back to what we were atlking about before  - the animated sequences via remotion - and think thorugh how this all fits together

> AGENT

For animated sequences via Remotion, the clean architecture is:

A **sequence** is a timeline clip that renders itself. It is not an effect layer, not a shot setting, and not just a prompt result.

It should fit into the video editor like this:

```json
{
  "id": "clip-42",
  "clipType": "image-carousel",
  "track": "V2",
  "at": 12,
  "hold": 6,
  "params": {
    "title": "Featured Works",
    "assetRefs": ["asset-1", "asset-2", "asset-3"],
    "beats": [
      { "assetRef": "asset-1", "start": 0, "duration": 2 },
      { "assetRef": "asset-2", "start": 2, "duration": 2 },
      { "assetRef": "asset-3", "start": 4, "duration": 2 }
    ],
    "layout": "stacked",
    "motion": "gentle-push"
  }
}
```

The timeline owns where it lives. The clip owns what it does internally.

**How It Fits Existing Systems**
`TimelineRenderer`
Already supports non-built-in `clipType` dispatch through `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`. That is the right path for sequence rendering.

`timeline-theme-2rp`
Already has examples: `section-hook`, `art-card`, `cta-card`, `resource-card`. These are animated sequences in practice. They just need a better frontend authoring surface.

`TimelineConfig.theme`
This is the video-wide design system. If a timeline has `theme: "2rp"`, the sequence creator should default to that theme.

`clip.params`
This is where generated sequence content belongs: title, copy, selected assets, beat timing, layout, local overrides.

`EffectCreatorPanel`
This is the closest existing UX pattern, but the concept is different. Effects wrap clips. Sequences create clips.

**Professional UX**
Add a third floating button beside Text and Effect:

- Text
- Effect
- Sequence

Clicking Sequence opens `SequenceCreatorPanel`.

Panel sections:

1. **Intent**
   A prompt field:
   “Create a 5-second animated intro using these selected images.”

2. **Assets**
   Attachment chips like AgentChat:
   - selected timeline clips
   - images/videos from gallery
   - shot images
   - uploaded media
   - drag to reorder
   - remove
   - role selector: hero / background / texture / reference

3. **Theme**
   Optional:
   - Auto
   - Timeline theme
   - None
   - Custom style prompt

   Style should not be required. If no style is selected, generate a clean neutral sequence.

4. **Sequence Type**
   - Auto
   - Title card
   - Image carousel
   - Art card
   - CTA
   - Resource card
   - Before/after
   - Quote card
   - Montage beat

5. **Timing**
   - Duration
   - Fit to selection range
   - Beat speed
   - per-asset timing table after generation

6. **Preview**
   Remotion preview of the generated draft.

7. **Actions**
   - Generate
   - Regenerate
   - Insert
   - Replace selected sequence
   - Save preset

**Generation Flow**
Do not generate arbitrary code by default.

Generate a structured `SequenceDraft` first:

```ts
type SequenceDraft = {
  clipType: string;
  theme?: string | null;
  durationSeconds: number;
  params: {
    title?: string;
    subtitle?: string;
    assetRefs?: string[];
    beats?: Array<{
      assetRef?: string;
      start: number;
      duration: number;
      text?: Record<string, string>;
      animation?: string;
    }>;
    layout?: string;
    motion?: string;
    styleOverride?: Record<string, unknown>;
  };
};
```

Then render that draft using known Remotion sequence components.

Only after the system is stable should there be an “Advanced custom sequence” path that generates new Remotion component code.

**Where Data Lives**
Before insert:
- local draft state in `SequenceCreatorPanel`

After insert:
- actual timeline clip in `TimelineConfig.clips`
- asset references in timeline asset registry
- content/timing in `clip.params`
- macro duration in `clip.hold`
- timeline-wide theme in `TimelineConfig.theme`

Optional reusable presets:
- `resources.type = "sequence-preset"`

Optional custom generated sequence code:
- `resources.type = "sequence-component"` or similar

**Shot/Project Style**
Use shot/project style only as input to generation, not as the sequence’s source of truth.

So the generator can say:
- use selected shot’s visual style
- use project style
- use timeline theme
- use no style

But once inserted, the clip is self-contained and reproducible.

**Editing After Insert**
The main timeline edits:
- position
- track
- duration
- split/delete/duplicate

The inspector edits:
- sequence text
- asset order
- beat timing
- layout
- animation style
- theme/style overrides

LLM edits:
- “make this punchier”
- “swap image 2 and 3”
- “make it 4 seconds”
- “turn it into a CTA”
- “use the selected image as the hero”

That gives both professional controls and natural language speed.

**Best Mental Model**
Effects are filters.

Sequences are clips.

Themes are design systems.

Assets are inputs.

Presets are reusable recipes.

The animated sequence feature should be a structured clip authoring tool powered by AI, not just an AI code generator and not just another effect panel.

That error means Remotion/browser is trying to treat `.txt` timeline assets as playable video sources. I’m going to trace where asset type/media kind is inferred and make sure non-media assets are excluded or rendered as non-video resources instead of reaching `<Video>`/MediaPlayer.

> AGENT

The stack trace points at `VisualClipSequence`, not the new resource-card materialization. A `.txt` asset has made it into a visual track with an asset entry that is not image, so the fallback path renders it through `<Video>`. I’m going to harden that render path and the drop/add path so text/non-media assets can’t be treated as videos.

I found two concrete holes: `buildAssetDropEdit()` treats any non-image visual-ish asset as video if it has duration or a visual-looking filename, and `VisualClip` falls back to `<Video>` for every non-image asset. I’m patching both so unsupported/text assets are ignored on drop and render as a labeled placeholder if already present in a saved timeline.

> AGENT

Fixed both issues and pushed.

What changed:
- `ai-generate-sequence` and `ai-generate-effect` now use `claude-opus-4-6` instead of the invalid dated model id.
- Deployed both Edge functions to Supabase.
- `.txt` / non-media assets are no longer added to the timeline as playable video/audio.
- Existing bad non-media clips now render as an unsupported-asset placeholder instead of crashing Remotion’s media player.
- Added regression test for rejecting text assets in `buildAssetDropEdit`.

Validation:
- Focused timeline/media tests passed: `28 passed`.
- Edge tests for `ai-generate-sequence` + `ai-generate-effect` passed: `12 passed`.
- TypeScript passed.
- Deployed `ai-generate-sequence` and `ai-generate-effect`.
- Pushed commit `38ccbad24 fix(video-editor): guard unsupported media assets` to both current branch and `main`.

The console error should stop after the frontend picks up this commit.

{"error":"Unexpected token 'T', \"The prompt\"... is not valid JSON"}

> AGENT

That means Anthropic returned prose starting with “The prompt…” instead of raw JSON, and the Edge function tried to parse it directly. I’m going to harden the sequence response extraction so it can recover JSON fenced blocks/objects and return a structured validation error instead of throwing raw JSON parse errors.

> AGENT

I’m going straight at the parse failure now. The edge function is trusting Anthropic to return raw JSON; I’m going to make the extractor accept fenced/prose-wrapped JSON and return a controlled error when there is no JSON at all.

The current extractor has exactly the brittle path from the error: it strips only whole-response fences, then `JSON.parse`s the entire response. I’m patching that to search for the first balanced JSON object/array inside the model text and adding regression tests around the bad response shape.

tool_use apply_patch
```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts\n@@\n interface LLMResponse {\n   content: string;\n   model: string;\n }\n+\n+const buildRepairMessages = (\n+  originalContent: string,\n+  allowedClipTypes: readonly string[],\n+  allowedAssetKeys: readonly string[],\n+): Array<{ role: string; content: string }> => [\n+  {\n+    role: \"system\",\n+    content: [\n+      \"You repair malformed Reigh sequence draft responses.\",\n+      \"Return JSON only, with shape {\\\"drafts\\\":[{\\\"clipType\\\":string,\\\"hold\\\":number,\\\"params\\\":object}]}.\",\n+      \"Do not include analysis, explanations, Markdown, or text before or after the JSON.\",\n+      \"Use only the allowed clipType values and allowed asset keys.\",\n+    ].join(\"\\n\"),\n+  },\n+  {\n+    role: \"user\",\n+    content: JSON.stringify({\n+      malformed_response: originalContent.slice(0, 8000),\n+      allowed_clip_types: allowedClipTypes,\n+      allowed_asset_keys: allowedAssetKeys,\n+    }),\n+  },\n+];\n@@\n     let rawDrafts: unknown[];\n     try {\n       rawDrafts = extractSequenceDrafts(llmResponse.content);\n     } catch (parseErr: unknown) {\n       const parseMsg = parseErr instanceof Error ? parseErr.message : String(parseErr);\n-      logger.info(`[AI-GENERATE-SEQUENCE] extraction failed: ${parseMsg}`);\n-      await logger.flush();\n-      return jsonResponse({ error: parseMsg }, 422);\n+      logger.info(`[AI-GENERATE-SEQUENCE] extraction failed: ${parseMsg}; retrying JSON repair`);\n+      await logger.flush();\n+      const repairResponse = await callAnthropic(\n+        buildRepairMessages(llmResponse.content, allowedClipTypes, allowedAssetKeys),\n+        logger,\n+      );\n+      try {\n+        rawDrafts = extractSequenceDrafts(repairResponse.content);\n+        llmResponse.content = repairResponse.content;\n+      } catch (repairErr: unknown) {\n+        const repairMsg = repairErr instanceof Error ? repairErr.message : String(repairErr);\n+        logger.info(`[AI-GENERATE-SEQUENCE] repair extraction failed: ${repairMsg}`);\n+        await logger.flush();\n+        return jsonResponse({ error: repairMsg }, 422);\n+      }\n     }\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

The console errors were two separate server responses:

`500`: from the old deployed model id `claude-opus-4-6-20260205`, which Anthropic rejected with 404. That was already fixed to `claude-opus-4-6`.

`422`: from Anthropic returning prose/no JSON instead of the expected sequence JSON. I had fixed prose-wrapped JSON parsing, but I just added a second repair pass too: if the first model response has no parseable JSON, the edge function calls Anthropic once more with a strict “repair this into JSON only” prompt before returning 422.

I’m going to trace the sequence metadata and prompt payload now. From what you describe, the generator is probably constrained to the current card-style sequence types where `title` is required, so “jump between images” gets squeezed into a resource/title card instead of an image motion sequence.

> AGENT

That is the root cause: this first version only exposes four trusted sequence components, and every one is a text/card component with `title` required except resource-card also supports images. So the model is not really free to answer “jump between these three images”; it has to pick a card schema and invent title text to pass validation.

> AGENT

That matches what I’m seeing in the code: the preview is rendering the whole timeline with a temporary sequence inserted, starting at the global playhead. That means it can keep playing into later timeline content instead of being a bounded preview of just the generated sequence. I’m going to make the sequence modal preview a small isolated timeline: one visual track, one draft clip at time 0, duration equal to the draft, with its own playback starting at 0.

> AGENT

I’ve added an `image-jump` sequence type with no title field, made the prompt prefer it for “jump between images”, cleared stale drafts at generation start, and isolated the modal preview to just the generated draft. I’m running the focused tests now because this touches validation, preview materialization, and renderer dispatch.

> AGENT

You were right: the generator was boxed into the wrong abstraction.

What was happening:
- The only available sequence types were text/card templates.
- Most required `title`, so “make it jump between these images” got forced into a title/resource-card shape.
- The modal did not clear old drafts before a new generation.
- The preview rendered the whole timeline with the draft inserted at the current playhead, so playback could continue into later timeline content.

I fixed that:
- Added a new `image-jump` sequence type: no title, image-only, animates selected/attached images.
- Updated the edge prompt to prefer `image-jump` for prompts like “jump between these images”.
- Updated edge validation to accept `image-jump` with `imageAssetKeys`.
- Cleared stale drafts immediately when a new generation starts.
- Made the modal preview isolated: it now previews only the generated draft from `0s`, not the rest of the timeline.
- Kept the preview controls already present on top of the Remotion preview.

Deployed:
- `ai-generate-sequence` deployed to Supabase.
- Smoke check returned `200 {"ok":true}`.
- Pushed commit `c578876ba fix(video-editor): add image motion sequence drafts` to both `main` and `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator`.

Verified:
- Frontend focused tests: 34 passed.
- Edge tests: 10 passed.
- Typecheck passed.

There are still unrelated `.megaplan/schemas/*` local changes, left uncommitted.

> DEVELOPER

and are we using the inert logic for the new clip that we use elsewhere? the positions it in the right place that's available?

> AGENT

I’ll check the sequence insertion path against the existing clip insertion helpers. The key question is whether `Insert at playhead` uses the same overlap/available-slot resolution as other timeline insertions, or whether it just drops at the requested time and lets overlap resolution move/trim it.

> AGENT

The answer is: partly, but not enough. Sequence insertion uses `getCompatibleTrackId` and `resolveOverlaps`, so it avoids hard overlaps, but it does not use the same nearest-free-track / snap-to-free-edge placement helpers that asset drops use. I’m going to patch `buildInsertSequenceDraftEdit` to use those helpers before falling back to overlap trimming.

> DEVELOPER

is this all excdellent engineered?

> AGENT

It is better engineered now, but I would not call the whole animated-sequence feature “excellent” yet.

What is solid:
- Edge function is deployed, tested, PAT/session-auth compatible, and has schema validation.
- Model output is constrained and repaired instead of blindly trusted.
- Sequence insertion now uses the existing timeline placement logic: snap to edge, nearest free visual track, then overlap resolution fallback.
- Preview is isolated to the generated draft instead of leaking into later timeline content.
- Stale drafts clear on regeneration.
- Save buttons are pinned inside the modal viewport.
- `image-jump` fixes the bad abstraction where image motion was forced into title/card templates.
- Recent fixes are pushed to `main`.

What is still not “excellent”:
- `image-jump` is a local Reigh sequence, not a proper generated theme-package clip. It is pragmatic and works, but longer term it should probably live in the timeline theme/package system with metadata generated from the same source as other sequences.
- The modal layout has been improved, but it has not been browser-screenshot verified across short laptop, desktop, and mobile-ish heights.
- The generator still depends on a narrow library of trusted sequence types. It is now less wrong, but not yet a broad professional sequence authoring system.
- We do not yet have a polished “draft lifecycle” UX: loading state, replacement of old drafts, error surface, and selected assets could be cleaner.

So: it is reasonably well-contained and production-safe for this iteration. It is not over-engineered, but it is still a first real pass rather than the final architecture.

> DEVELOPER

why when i type a new prompt does the same animation appear again? IS it definitely generating a new one? Whne one has been generated can you put the ability to edit or regenerate from scratch? Make it should switch to edit but they should be able to switch back to generate. And we should name the existing animations they've done based on their query

> AGENT

I’ll tighten the interaction model here. Right now a new prompt can still look identical because the same draft type and params are selected again, and the UI does not clearly distinguish “generate a new animation” from “edit/regenerate the selected one.” I’m going to add explicit generate/edit modes and label generated draft groups from the prompt so it’s obvious what came from which query.

> AGENT

I found the core issue in the UI state: there is only one anonymous `drafts` array, so a new generation replaces it but the panel doesn’t tell you whether you’re editing the current result or starting a fresh animation. I’m changing it to named animation runs keyed off the prompt, with an explicit Generate/Edit switch and a “Regenerate from scratch” path for the selected run.

it doesn't seem to be respecting the request, are you sure there's not something overriding it? or some reason why tehy'ere all the same? Just items jumping in on top of the the other? Also, there are two Xs in the top right?

> AGENT

TypeScript is clean. I’m going deeper on the “all the same” behavior now: checking the trusted sequence schema and Remotion component because if the schema only exposes one motion pattern, the model can’t meaningfully honor requests beyond choosing that same jump preset. Also checking the duplicate close buttons in the modal header.

> AGENT

Root cause found: the schema allowed `mode`, but the Remotion component only treated `gallery` differently for image fit; every other request rendered the same jump/pop animation. I’m adding real motion variants so prompts like gallery/shuffle/pulse/snap have visible differences instead of all collapsing to the same “one image jumps on top” behavior.

> AGENT

Pushed to both `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator` and `main`.

What changed:
- Allowed Assets now reuses the timeline chat attachment strip, so images/videos display like the chat window.
- Removed the duplicate custom `X`; modal now only uses the built-in dialog close button.
- Added hover text for the three timeline buttons.
- Added `Create animation sequence` to clip right-click menus for single/multi selections.
- Edit mode now edits the selected animation via an instruction field instead of “regenerate from scratch.”
- Fixed the “everything looks the same” issue by making `image-jump` modes actually render differently: `jump`, `snap`, `gallery`, `pulse`, `shuffle`.
- Edge prompt now tells the model to choose those modes instead of always returning `jump`.

Verified:
- `npx vitest run ...` focused suite: `60 passed`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck`: clean
- Deployed `ai-generate-sequence` to Supabase project `wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`.

Commit: `8ecc97523 fix(video-editor): refine sequence creator workflow`

> AGENT

Done. I ran a light megaplan cleanup and implemented the important parts.

What I changed:
- `Replace selected` now replaces the full selected visual set with one sequence clip, not just the primary clip.
- The inserted replacement is selected correctly after the old clips are deleted.
- Allowed Assets now has the same removable X behavior as the timeline chat attachment strip.
- Image-jump `mode` is now constrained across client metadata, client validation, edge validation, prompt guidance, and tests.
- Added regression tests for multi-selection replacement, asset X removal, stale draft clearing, invalid edit preservation, and sequence materialization.

Verified:
- Focused Vitest suite: `52 passed`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck`: clean
- Redeployed `ai-generate-sequence` to Supabase.
- Pushed commit `ad9f440ec fix(video-editor): harden sequence creator replacement` to both `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator` and `main`.

Only remaining dirty files are the pre-existing unrelated `.megaplan/schemas/*` files; I left them out.

viewportWidth);\n+    const maxScrollTop = Math.max(0, scrollContentHeight - viewportHeight);\n+    const nextScrollLeft = clamp(clipCenterX - viewportWidth / 2, 0, maxScrollLeft);\n+    const nextScrollTop = clamp(clipCenterY - viewportHeight / 2, 0, maxScrollTop);\n+\n+    container.scrollTo({\n+      left: nextScrollLeft,\n+      top: nextScrollTop,\n+      behavior: 'smooth',\n+    });\n+    return true;\n+  }, [pixelsPerSecond, rowHeight, rows, scrollContentHeight, startLeft, totalWidth]);\n+\n+  const scheduleCenterClipInViewport = useCallback((clipId: string) => {\n+    pendingCenterClipIdRef.current = clipId;\n+    requestAnimationFrame(() => {\n+      requestAnimationFrame(() => {\n+        if (pendingCenterClipIdRef.current !== clipId) {\n+          return;\n+        }\n+        if (centerClipInViewport(clipId)) {\n+          pendingCenterClipIdRef.current = null;\n+        }\n+      });\n+    });\n+  }, [centerClipInViewport]);\n+\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    const handleCenterClip = (event: Event) => {\n+      const detail = (event as CustomEvent<TimelineCenterClipEventDetail>).detail;\n+      if (!detail?.clipId) {\n+        return;\n+      }\n+      scheduleCenterClipInViewport(detail.clipId);\n+    };\n+    window.addEventListener(TIMELINE_CENTER_CLIP_EVENT, handleCenterClip);\n+    return () => window.removeEventListener(TIMELINE_CENTER_CLIP_EVENT, handleCenterClip);\n+  }, [scheduleCenterClipInViewport]);\n+\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    const pendingClipId = pendingCenterClipIdRef.current;\n+    if (pendingClipId) {\n+      scheduleCenterClipInViewport(pendingClipId);\n+    }\n+  }, [rows, scheduleCenterClipInViewport]);\n*** End Patch\n"

> AGENT

No, not exactly. It’s the same **timeline-family structure**, but Reigh’s new animation sequence path is a stricter, curated subset.

ArtAgents’ canonical timeline shape already mirrors Reigh: `clipType`, `hold`, `params`, `entrance`, `exit`, `continuous`, `transition`, etc. See [artagents/timeline.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/timeline.py:2) and the allowed clip keys at [artagents/timeline.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/timeline.py:346).

But ArtAgents supports broader animation/effect semantics:

- Built-ins are `media`, `hold`, `text`, `effect-layer`; custom theme effects are accepted as direct `clipType` values if registered, e.g. `section-hook`, `resource-card` ([artagents/timeline.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/timeline.py:134), [artagents/timeline.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/timeline.py:889)).
- ArtAgents permits effect params to contain animation references like `entrance`, `sustain`, and `exit`, then validates those against the animation catalog ([artagents/timeline.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/timeline.py:532)).
- For branded effects, ArtAgents explicitly strips LLM-proposed `entrance`, `exit`, and `sustain`, because theme defaults should own styling ([artagents/cut.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/cut.py:118)).
- ArtAgents materializes branded generative overlays as direct effect clip types: `{ clipType: effect_id, hold, params }` ([artagents/cut.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/cut.py:552)).

Reigh’s sequence creator currently accepts only trusted sequence drafts shaped like:

```json
{
  "clipType": "image-jump",
  "hold": 4,
  "params": {
    "imageAssetKeys": ["asset-a", "asset-b"],
    "mode": "shuffle"
  }
}
```

That trusted set is defined in [metadata.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/sequences/metadata.ts:41): `image-jump`, `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, `cta-card`. Its validator only allows top-level `clipType`, `hold`, and `params`, and rejects generated code plus animation refs such as `entrance`, `exit`, `transition`, `animationRefs` ([validation.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts:32)).

The main mismatch is `image-jump`: I don’t see ArtAgents using that effect id, while Reigh now treats it as a first-class trusted sequence. The other four branded effects overlap conceptually with ArtAgents’ `2rp` effects.

One more nuance: ArtAgents’ local Remotion root only directly handles `media`, `text`, and `effect-layer` kinds like `ados-card` ([Root.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/remotion/src/Root.tsx:162)). The direct theme effect clip types are validated/generated in the schema/catalog path, not rendered by that local bespoke branch.

So the answer is: compatible outer structure, not exact same animation-effect structure. Reigh’s sequence creator is deliberately narrower and safer than ArtAgents’ broader timeline/effect model. If we want real parity, `image-jump` should be added to the shared/catalog side or the trusted sequence metadata should be generated from the same theme package contract instead of handwritten in Reigh.

```ts
generation: {
  source: 'sequence_creator',

  prompt,
```

> DEVELOPER

and do we have flfexibility to do ALL kinds of animations with that sequence thing? Is it extremely flexible?

> AGENT

No. It is deliberately **not** extremely flexible yet.

Right now the sequence thing is a **trusted structured generator**, not a freeform animation/code generator. It can generate only the trusted clip types we expose:

`image-jump`, `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, `cta-card`

That gives us good safety and predictable rendering, but it limits animation range. The model cannot currently invent arbitrary Remotion code, new layouts, new motion systems, transitions, or animation refs. In fact, the validator explicitly rejects code, JSX, raw URLs, `entrance`, `exit`, `transition`, and animation refs.

So the flexibility is:

- **Good for:** choosing between known animation components, filling params, selecting assets, changing timing, using image lists, editing text/content.
- **Not good for:** “make any animation I can imagine”, custom choreography, arbitrary compositions, new effect code, complex multi-beat timelines, camera systems, masks, kinetic typography systems, etc.

To make it truly flexible while staying well engineered, I’d add a middle layer:

1. Keep the current trusted sequence path for safe common animations.
2. Add a richer declarative animation schema, not raw code:
   - scenes/beats
   - asset slots
   - layout primitives
   - motion primitives
   - timing/keyframes
   - text layers
   - transitions
   - theme/style references
3. Render that schema through a small set of robust Remotion primitives.
4. Let LLM edit that schema, not generate code.
5. Only later allow code-generated sequences behind stricter sandboxing/review.

So: it’s flexible enough for curated professional sequence types, but not yet flexible enough for “all kinds of animations.”

> DEVELOPER

Could the LLM edit that schema AND generate code? How could we make it maxmimally flexible? How could that work in ArtAgents?

> AGENT

Yes. The strongest version is a two-lane system:

**Lane 1: declarative schema**
The LLM edits a structured animation document: assets, layers, beats, timing, keyframes, easing, masks, text, camera moves, transitions, constraints. This is safe, inspectable, diffable, and easy to re-edit.

**Lane 2: generated Remotion module**
When the schema is not expressive enough, the LLM can generate a Remotion component, but only inside a constrained package contract.

A generated sequence would look roughly like:

```json
{
  "clipType": "generated-sequence",
  "hold": 6,
  "params": {
    "schema": { "...declarative animation plan..." },
    "moduleId": "seq_abc123",
    "assetKeys": ["asset-a", "asset-b"]
  },
  "generation": {
    "sequence_creator": {
      "prompt": "make these images orbit then snap into a stack",
      "mode": "code+schema"
    }
  }
}
```

For maximum flexibility, I would make the schema the source of truth and code the compiled/renderable artifact. The LLM can update both, but we validate the schema first and compile/render-test the code before allowing insert.

In ArtAgents, this fits naturally:

- ArtAgents can produce the same `generated-sequence` clip type in timeline JSON.
- It can store generated Remotion code as an artifact in the run directory.
- The timeline clip references that artifact by `moduleId`.
- The render step resolves `moduleId` to a compiled component.
- Editor/review loops can ask for changes against the schema and code artifact.

The contract should be strict:

- No arbitrary imports except an allowlist: `react`, `remotion`, approved local helpers.
- No network, DOM mutation, timers, random without seeded helper, filesystem, eval, dynamic import.
- Component receives only `{ clip, params, assets, theme, fps }`.
- Asset usage must go through registry keys.
- Typecheck generated code.
- Run a render smoke test.
- Optional screenshot/frame checks before insertion.
- Store prompt, schema, code hash, validation status, and model in `generation.sequence_creator`.

A robust pipeline would be:

1. User prompt + selected assets.
2. LLM proposes declarative animation schema.
3. Validator checks schema, asset keys, duration, bounds.
4. If schema primitives cover it, render with built-in `schema-sequence`.
5. If not, LLM generates a Remotion component implementing that schema.
6. Typecheck and sandbox-validate the code.
7. Render preview frames.
8. Insert only if validation passes.
9. Later edits modify schema first, then regenerate/patch code if needed.

For Reigh app, this means adding one or two new trusted clip types:

- `schema-sequence`: no generated code, just declarative animation JSON.
- `generated-sequence`: declarative schema plus compiled/generated Remotion module reference.

For ArtAgents, it means adding the same clip types to the effect/catalog layer and teaching the renderer how to resolve generated modules from a run artifact directory or shared package cache.

That gives max flexibility without turning the timeline into raw untrusted code. The timeline stays structured, portable, and ArtAgents-compatible; the generated code becomes a validated implementation detail.

> DEVELOPER

And will the data work for that Will it work nicely with teh render engine generally? How will we need to change the edge function?

> AGENT

Yes, the data can work, but not with only the current `clipType + hold + params` whitelist. We need one more layer of structure.

The clean render-engine shape is:

```json
{
  "id": "clip_123",
  "at": 12,
  "track": "V2",
  "clipType": "schema-sequence",
  "hold": 6,
  "params": {
    "version": 1,
    "assets": ["asset-a", "asset-b"],
    "scene": {
      "layers": [],
      "beats": [],
      "camera": {},
      "transitions": []
    }
  },
  "generation": {
    "sequence_creator": {
      "prompt": "...",
      "mode": "schema",
      "model": "...",
      "schema_hash": "..."
    }
  }
}
```

For generated code:

```json
{
  "clipType": "generated-sequence",
  "hold": 6,
  "params": {
    "version": 1,
    "assets": ["asset-a"],
    "schema": {},
    "moduleId": "seq_abc123",
    "moduleHash": "sha256..."
  }
}
```

That works nicely with the render engine if we make the renderer resolve by `clipType`:

- `schema-sequence` goes to a built-in Remotion component that interprets the schema.
- `generated-sequence` goes to a generated-module registry, loads the compiled component by `moduleId`, and passes `{ clip, params, assets, theme, fps }`.
- Asset URLs still come from registry keys, not from model-emitted URLs.
- `generation.sequence_creator` remains provenance only, not render input.

The edge function should change from “return trusted drafts only” to a staged generator:

1. Accept prompt, selected assets, timeline context, theme.
2. Return either:
   - `schema` draft for `schema-sequence`, or
   - `schema + code` draft for `generated-sequence`.
3. Validate the schema server-side.
4. If code is included, run code validation:
   - import allowlist
   - no browser/network/fs/eval/dynamic import
   - TypeScript compile
   - exported component contract
5. Store generated code as an artifact, probably Supabase Storage or DB table.
6. Return only validated insertable drafts to the client.

So the edge response becomes something like:

```json
{
  "drafts": [
    {
      "clipType": "schema-sequence",
      "hold": 6,
      "params": {
        "version": 1,
        "assets": ["asset-a", "asset-b"],
        "scene": {}
      },
      "generation": {
        "sequence_creator": {
          "prompt": "...",
          "mode": "schema"
        }
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

And for code:

```json
{
  "drafts": [
    {
      "clipType": "generated-sequence",
      "hold": 6,
      "params": {
        "version": 1,
        "assets": ["asset-a"],
        "schema": {},
        "moduleId": "seq_abc123",
        "moduleHash": "..."
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

Main changes needed:

- Add `schema-sequence` and maybe `generated-sequence` to trusted sequence metadata.
- Add validators for nested animation schema.
- Add a Remotion `SchemaSequence` renderer.
- Add generated module storage/registry if we allow code.
- Update edge prompt to generate schema first, code only when necessary.
- Update edge validation to reject unsafe code and invalid schema.
- Update ArtAgents to accept/render the same clip types.

I would not let the edge function just return arbitrary code to the browser and insert it. The browser/editor should only ever receive validated drafts and stable module references.

> AGENT

Yes. The plan seems high-quality.

The reasons that matter:

- It preserves the current safe `ai-generate-sequence` path instead of weakening it.
- It splits flexibility into sane lanes: trusted structured sequences, declarative schema sequences, and generated Remotion modules.
- It treats generated code as a worker/artifact concern, not browser-executed editor code.
- It explicitly covers Reigh app, the edge function, render routing, persistence, `timeline-import`, ArtAgents, and the shared schema package.
- It caught a real routing hazard: unknown generated clip types would otherwise fall back to the client renderer.
- It caught a real schema hazard: Reigh local timeline types and `@banodoco/timeline-schema` currently disagree.
- It now names the concrete shared-schema source/codegen/test files, so implementation won’t rely on vague “update the schema package” instructions.

The biggest remaining product decisions before implementation are: first rollout lane, dependency policy for generated modules, and exactly how `generation_defaults` should be represented in the aligned shared schema.

> DEVELOPER

'Motion
Motion style. Use jump for hard cuts, snap for quick scale hits, gallery for side-by-side browsing, pulse for rhythmic zooms, and shuffle for overlapping card movement.' what does this mean?

> AGENT

That copy is too implementation-ish.

It means the `image-jump` sequence has a `mode` param that chooses the animation preset:

- `jump`: hard cuts between images
- `snap`: quick scale/pop transitions
- `gallery`: shows images side-by-side or in a browse layout
- `pulse`: rhythmic zoom in/out
- `shuffle`: overlapping cards moving around

But showing that whole explanation in the UI is clunky. Better UI copy:

**Label:** `Motion style`

**Description:** `Choose how the selected images move through the sequence.`

Then the dropdown/options should show human labels:

- `Jump cuts`
- `Snap pops`
- `Gallery`
- `Pulse zooms`
- `Shuffle cards`

The model/prompt can keep the detailed explanation internally, but the user-facing field should be shorter and more natural.

> DEVELOPER

but why is that evne resitrcted? Could we not dnymaically programme that or choose from templates if it fits?

> AGENT

It’s restricted because the current `image-jump` path is not a general animation engine. It is a trusted component with a small `mode` enum, so the LLM can only fill safe params that the renderer already knows how to draw.

That restriction buys us:

- predictable rendering
- no broken generated code in the editor
- easy validation
- safe asset handling
- stable timeline persistence
- fast preview

But yes, we can make it much more dynamic.

The better model is:

1. **Template when it fits**
   The LLM chooses a known sequence template like `image-jump`, `resource-card`, `section-hook`, etc. Good for common cases.

2. **Declarative animation schema when templates don’t fit**
   The LLM outputs structured animation data: layers, keyframes, timing, easing, transforms, masks, asset refs, text, transitions. A generic Remotion renderer interprets it.

3. **Generated Remotion module when schema still isn’t enough**
   The LLM generates code, but it is compiled/validated server-side and rendered in a worker, not executed raw in the browser.

So instead of forcing `mode: jump | snap | gallery | pulse | shuffle`, the future version could let the LLM produce something like:

```json
{
  "layers": [
    {
      "assetKey": "image-a",
      "keyframes": [
        { "time": 0, "x": -200, "scale": 0.8, "opacity": 0 },
        { "time": 0.4, "x": 0, "scale": 1.1, "opacity": 1 },
        { "time": 1.2, "x": 80, "scale": 1, "opacity": 1 }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

Then if the request is simple, use a template. If it’s specific, use schema. If it’s too expressive for schema, generate a Remotion module.

The current restriction is a safe v1, not where this should end.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Yes. Then we should stop thinking of this as “choose one of five modes.”

**Maximum power architecture:**

1. **Template match first**
   If the prompt fits a known high-quality template, use it. This gives fast, reliable results.

2. **Dynamic declarative animation**
   If the prompt is more specific, the LLM writes an animation schema: layers, assets, text, timing, transforms, keyframes, masks, easing, transitions, camera moves.

3. **Generated Remotion code**
   If schema is still too limiting, generate a Remotion module, validate/compile it server-side, render it in worker, and preview the result. Not raw browser execution.

4. **Editable after generation**
   User says: “make it faster,” “more chaotic,” “use image 3 as the hero,” “make the images orbit,” etc. LLM edits the schema/code artifact.

So the UI should not expose “jump/snap/gallery/pulse/shuffle” as the main idea. That can remain an internal shortcut or optional advanced template selection.

The user-facing model should be:

> “Describe the animation you want.”

Then Reigh decides:
- template
- schema
- generated module

For now, we should remove/soften the visible restriction and build toward the schema/code system in the architecture plan. Current Megaplan doc execution is at **8/9 tasks complete**, so it’s nearly done.

> AGENT

Yes. Current unnecessary restrictions:

- `Motion style` enum: should become optional “simple preset,” not the core path.
- Fixed trusted clip types only: good as safe fallback, but not enough for real animation creation.
- Asset-count caps like `8/8`: okay for simple templates, but dynamic schema/code lanes should support larger scenes with performance guards.
- Required text fields on cards: should not force title/caption when the user asks for pure motion.
- No animation refs/code in v1: still correct for v1, but we need separate schema/code lanes instead of broadening v1.
- Asset keys only from selected/attached: correct for safety, but UX should make adding more assets easy.
- Raw URLs blocked: correct for compatibility/security. We should import/register URLs as assets, not put URLs directly in params.

The product shape should be:

**Simple mode:** choose from templates/presets if the prompt fits.

**Power mode:** user just describes anything; Reigh chooses template, declarative schema, or generated Remotion module.

**Compatibility layer:** every result compiles back to canonical `TimelineConfig` + `AssetRegistry`, with `generation.sequence_lane` metadata, so Reigh, workers, `timeline-import`, and ArtAgents can all understand it.

Also: the Megaplan doc is now done. Review completed with **14/15 criteria passed**, no rework items. The remaining miss is informational/product-decision level, not an implementation blocker.

> AGENT

That field was a bad UX leak. “Animation intent” is useful as an internal edge-function hint, but it should not be a second user-facing prompt competing with “Prompt” or “Edit”. The user should describe the animation once.

I fixed it in [SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx):

- Removed the visible `Animation Intent` fields from generate and edit modes.
- Removed the separate `animationIntent` / `editAnimationIntent` React state.
- The normal prompt/edit instruction now becomes the internal `animation_intent.freeform`.
- Provenance metadata still stores sanitized intent, so URLs get redacted before saving into clip metadata.

Updated tests in [SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx) to assert this behavior.

Verification passed:

- `npx vitest run ...SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx` -> 15 passed
- Broader focused animation/render tests -> 49 passed
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` -> passed
- Megaplan implementation is marked `done`; review succeeded.

> AGENT

Not “all excellent” yet, but the important structure is now good.

The solid parts:

- Generated sequence lanes are centralized in `generated-lanes.ts`.
- Browser render blocking for future `remotion_module` clips is explicit and tested.
- Sequence generation uses one user prompt, with `animation_intent` kept internal.
- Clip provenance is namespaced under `generation.sequence_creator`.
- Tests cover the core paths: generation, edit, insert, replace, render routing, preview blocking, edge prompt behavior.
- TypeScript passes.

The parts I would still improve before calling it excellent:

- `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx` is doing too much: modal UI, prompt state, request orchestration, draft group management, metadata attachment, insert/replace actions. It should probably split into `useSequenceCreatorGeneration`, `useSequenceDraftGroups`, and smaller presentational panels.
- `SequenceAnimationIntent` should not live inside the component long term. It should move near shared sequence/generation types so edge payload, provenance, and UI agree.
- The edge function schema/prompt is better, but the compatibility contract should eventually be represented as a typed shared schema, not just tests plus prompt text.
- I ran `desloppify scan`; the repo’s mechanical score is strong overall, but the scan found broad existing repo debt and test-health issues. That is not specifically this feature failing, but it means “excellent across the whole repo” is not true.

So: the current implementation is professionally structured and tested for the trusted-v1 path. I would call it solid, not yet excellent. The next excellence pass is mostly extraction and type-boundary cleanup, not behavior rewriting.

> DEVELOPER

let's do a megaplan to clean it up, light or standard?

> DEVELOPER

Are you confident in those rcommendations?

> AGENT

Yes, confident in the recommendations, with one nuance.

The strongest recommendation is splitting `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`. That component is already mixing too many responsibilities: UI, request building, draft state, validation display, insert/replace mutation flow, provenance metadata, and mode switching. It works, but that is exactly the shape that gets brittle as soon as we add more sequence power.

I’m also confident `SequenceAnimationIntent`/generation provenance should move out of the component into shared sequence/generation types. It is now a real contract between UI, edge function, saved clip metadata, and future ArtAgents compatibility. Keeping that type local is fine for a quick iteration, not for a durable feature.

The one I’d treat carefully is “shared schema” cleanup. It is correct directionally, but we should avoid overbuilding it in this cleanup pass. I’d do a light/standard cleanup that creates a clean boundary and shared local types first, then only introduce broader package/schema sharing when ArtAgents integration actually consumes it.

For this cleanup I’d choose **light megaplan** if the goal is refactor without changing behavior. Choose **standard** only if we also want to harden the edge function contract and align ArtAgents at the same time. My recommendation: **light megaplan now**, because the behavioral surface is fresh and we want a clean extraction pass without reopening the whole architecture.

> DEVELOPER

When i drag an item to a point should the play head move tehre?

> AGENT

Usually, **yes, after the drop**, not continuously during the drag.

For this editor context I’d make it behave like this:

- While dragging: show the insertion/ghost position, but don’t move playback or scrub the preview.
- On drop: select the moved/inserted clip and move the playhead to the clip’s new start time.
- Also center or reveal that clip in the timeline viewport, same as insert/replace.
- If the drag is cancelled: leave the playhead where it was.

Reason: the playhead is the user’s “current inspection point.” Once they place something, they almost always want to inspect that exact placement. But moving it during drag can feel noisy and can accidentally scrub video/audio while they’re just positioning.

For edge cases:

- Dragging a clip duration edge: move playhead to the edited edge only after release, maybe start edge for trim-left and end edge for trim-right.
- Multi-select drag: move playhead to the earliest selected clip start after drop.
- Holding a modifier could preserve current playhead later, but I wouldn’t add that unless users ask.

> DEVELOPER

For multiple clips, where they land?

> AGENT

For multiple clips, move the playhead to the **earliest start time of the moved group after drop**.

So if you drag 3 selected clips and they land at:

- clip A: `12.0s`
- clip B: `14.5s`
- clip C: `18.0s`

the playhead should go to `12.0s`.

That gives the user the beginning of the newly placed arrangement, not an arbitrary individual clip. Also select/keep selected all moved clips, and center the viewport around the group’s start or bounding range.
